The Finance Manager should establish clear access controls, controlled storage locations, defined sharing permissions, and appropriate handling procedures for sensitive information. SharePoint permissions should reflect role requirements rather than broad convenience access. Audit responses should be shared only with relevant stakeholders, and working versions should be distinguished from approved records. The Finance Manager should also avoid placing confidential information into uncontrolled personal spreadsheets or unapproved communication channels. Where automation or AI tools are considered, the manager should ensure the tool is approved for the information involved. Power Query can operate within controlled source locations and preserve data lineage. VBA can support controlled outputs but should not be used to circumvent information-access restrictions. Confidentiality is therefore part of financial governance rather than simply a personal conduct expectation.

Send to the team:
- "Please store and share audit, donor, and sensitive financial information only through approved systems and according to the applicable access controls."
Key point:
- Information security supports financial accountability.
- Automation must operate within approved data-access and confidentiality controls.

38. Q: How would you support the use of AI or automated tools for audit readiness and document completeness while keeping accountability with Finance?
A: Show answer
The grants-compliance vacancy specifically mentions liaison with the IS team on AI-enabled audit readiness and automated checking of country SharePoint folders. The appropriate approach is to use automation to identify likely gaps, not to assume that an automated result is a final compliance conclusion. The Finance Manager should define the control criteria, determine what evidence the automated check is expected to find, test false positives and false negatives, and establish a human review step. For example, an automated tool might identify that a required document appears absent from a folder, but a Finance reviewer may need to determine whether an approved alternative document exists or whether the requirement applies to that project. The manager should document the logic, review results, exceptions, and override decisions. Power Query can support structured completeness testing where metadata is available, while VBA can produce exception logs and management summaries. AI tools should be used only within organizationally approved arrangements and appropriate confidentiality controls.

Send to the team:
- "Please treat AI or automated completeness checks as exception-identification tools and require human review before closing a compliance issue."
Key point:
- Automation can improve detection but does not replace financial accountability.
- Human review remains necessary for substantive compliance interpretation.

39. Q: How would you respond when a donor requirement is unclear and different teams have interpreted it differently?
A: Show answer
The grants-compliance vacancy requires the Finance Manager to interpret donor regulations and requirements, while the FCDO role requires expert advice on programme and financial management and compliance. The Finance Manager should first return to the authoritative funding agreement, amendment, guidance, or other applicable donor instruction and identify the exact provision at issue. The differing interpretations should then be documented, including the financial transactions or reporting consequences. Where the wording remains ambiguous, the matter should be escalated to the appropriate grants, programme, legal, donor, or senior-management authority rather than resolved informally by assumption. The resulting interpretation should be documented and, where material, incorporated into the donor compliance knowledge hub and relevant team guidance so the issue is not repeatedly re-litigated. Power Query or VBA may help identify transactions affected by the interpretation, but they cannot determine the contractual meaning. The Finance Manager should distinguish fact, documented interpretation, and management decision throughout the process.

Send to the team:
- "Please document the source of every material donor interpretation and escalate unresolved ambiguity before approving affected expenditure or reporting treatment."

Training should explain the downstream financial consequence of operational actions. Power Query can combine information from different functional trackers where consistent fields exist. This approach makes accountability visible and reduces the risk that a donor compliance failure is discovered only after Finance receives the final documentation.

44. Q: How would you manage competing priorities when an audit, donor report, portfolio forecast, and senior leadership request all arrive at the same time?
A: Show answer
Both vacancies require prioritization, organization, and delivery to tight deadlines. The Finance Manager should assess each request based on deadline, financial or compliance consequence, dependency, decision importance, and risk of delay. Work should be sequenced so that tasks sharing the same underlying financial data are coordinated rather than performed independently. Critical-path items should be identified early, and management should be informed when competing deadlines create a genuine capacity constraint. The manager should protect review time and avoid solving every urgent request through manual rework. Power Query can automate repetitive consolidation, while VBA can generate recurring packs and status reports, freeing capacity for substantive analysis. A senior Finance Manager should also distinguish between genuine urgency and requests that can be scheduled. The strongest response to pressure is a controlled prioritization process supported by clear communication and evidence-based escalation, not simply asking staff to work faster.

45. Q: How would you report an emerging financial risk to senior leadership without overstating the evidence?
A: Show answer
Both vacancies emphasize financial-risk management, analytical reporting, and senior leadership support. The Finance Manager should separate confirmed facts, current estimates, assumptions, and potential consequences. A risk report should state what has happened, the financial exposure or range where supportable, the programme or compliance implication, the evidence supporting the assessment, the mitigation already underway, and what decision or escalation is required. The manager should avoid presenting an uncertain forecast as a confirmed loss or using dramatic language unsupported by evidence. Where the information is incomplete, that limitation should be visible. Power Query can help ensure the analysis is based on the latest approved data, while VBA can standardize risk-reporting templates and track actions. Senior leadership should receive enough information to act but should not be misled into believing that a preliminary indicator is a final conclusion.

Send to the team:
- "Please distinguish confirmed financial facts from forecasts, assumptions, and potential exposures in every material risk escalation."
Key point:
- Senior risk reporting must be evidence-based and transparent about uncertainty.
- Management action should be linked to the specific risk exposure.

46. Q: How would you use audit findings and financial analysis together to strengthen risk management?
A: Show answer
Audit findings provide evidence of control performance, while financial analysis shows current and emerging exposure. The first vacancy requires audit findings, lessons learned, risk assessment, and corrective actions, while the second requires financial risk oversight and programme assurance. The Finance Manager should bring these information streams together. For example, a repeated documentation finding in one part of the portfolio may warrant closer monitoring of current expenditure, additional training, or targeted assurance. Conversely, a major forecast movement in a programme with strong controls may represent a financial issue without an underlying control failure. Management reporting should therefore distinguish control risk from financial performance risk while identifying where they interact. Power Query can combine risk registers, audit findings, financial data, and action status for trend analysis. VBA can generate integrated management reports. This creates a forward-looking control environment because historical findings inform where current financial attention should be concentrated.

Send to the team:
- "Please review current financial trends together with open and recurring audit findings when assessing programme risk."
Key point:
- Audit history can improve current risk targeting.
- Financial exposure and control weakness should be analyzed separately and together where they interact.

47. Q: How would you improve a weak finance process without creating a new bureaucracy around it?
A: Show answer
Both vacancies emphasize continuous improvement, strengthening systems, improving documentation, building capability, and promoting best practice. The Finance Manager should begin by identifying the actual failure point: duplicated data entry, unclear ownership, missing information, excessive review steps, inconsistent templates, or lack of timely reporting. The redesign should remove unnecessary manual effort while strengthening the control points that matter. A process map should show the source data, responsible owner, review points, approval, output, and evidence retained. Power Query can reduce repeated data consolidation, while VBA can automate recurring reports, checklists, and exception tracking. However, automation should not reproduce a bad process faster. The manager should pilot the revised process, compare error rates and turnaround time, collect feedback, and formally update procedures or guidance where the change becomes permanent. Continuous improvement should produce measurable gains in control quality, timeliness, usability, or management visibility.

Send to the team:
- "Please identify the actual control failure or bottleneck before adding a new form, tracker, or approval step."
Key point:
- Process improvement should simplify work while strengthening material controls.
- Automation is most useful after the underlying process is sound.

48. Q: How would you maintain a strong control environment when programmes operate under different donor requirements and reporting frameworks?

The grants-compliance vacancy makes donor-rule interpretation and consistent compliance practices a central responsibility, while the FCDO role requires compliance with a specific programme management framework and corporate assurance requirements. The Finance Manager should establish a common control architecture covering fundamental financial disciplines such as authorized expenditure, evidence, reporting, reconciliation, review, escalation, and record retention, then add donor- or programme-specific controls where the agreement requires them. This avoids having completely different finance processes for every donor while still respecting contractual differences. The compliance knowledge hub can document these variations. Power Query can use common data structures across grants even when reporting categories differ, while VBA can generate donor-specific checklists from a central rules database. The manager should periodically review whether changes in donor agreements, programme frameworks, or internal policy require updates to the control design. Consistency should mean consistent control principles and documentation, not pretending every donor requirement is identical.

Send to the team:

1. Q: How would you establish a financial management framework for a donor-funded program operating under both organizational and donor policies?
A: The Finance Manager should translate the program manual, organizational finance policies, donor requirements, approved budgets, delegated authorities, and applicable local law into one practical control framework. For the P4I-type environment, this means ensuring program payments, cash management, procurement interfaces, payroll, taxation, reporting, and record retention consistently follow both DT Global and DFAT requirements. For an FHI 360 district-based portfolio, the framework must also align transactions with FHI 360 procedures, project requirements, Indonesia regulations, and the accounting system. The manager should maintain a policy matrix showing the governing rule, responsible person, approval level, required evidence, accounting treatment, and escalation route. Staff training should then focus on the procedures that affect daily transactions rather than simply circulating policy documents. Power Query can consolidate transaction populations for control testing, while VBA can produce policy-compliance checklists and monthly exception summaries.
Send to the team:
- "Please map each major finance process to the applicable organizational, donor, legal, approval, and documentation requirements before transactions are processed."
Key point:
- A donor-funded control framework must connect policy, law, approvals, evidence, and accounting treatment.
- Procedures should be translated into repeatable operating controls, not left as reference documents.

2. Q: Why is transparent bookkeeping particularly important in donor-funded NGO programs?
A: Transparent bookkeeping provides the audit trail that connects an approved activity to a payment, supporting document, accounting entry, project code, budget line, and donor report. In a program such as P4I, the finance function must demonstrate that spending followed DFAT and program requirements and that records are complete enough for internal and external audit. In the FHI 360 role, timely and accurate transaction recording is specifically linked to project financial monitoring and reporting. The Finance Manager should therefore require complete source documents, consistent coding, documented approvals, and timely posting, while reviewing unusual or incomplete transactions before close. The aim is not simply to have a ledger that balances. It is to make every material transaction explainable from original evidence through management reporting.
Send to the team:
- "Please ensure every transaction can be traced from source document to accounting entry, project code, approval, and final report."
Key point:
- Transparent books create a defensible audit trail.
- Complete evidence is as important as correct arithmetic.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
3. Q: How would you manage day-to-day expenditure while preserving strong financial control?
A: Day-to-day expenditure requires a balance between responsive program implementation and disciplined control. The Finance Manager should establish a clear workflow from request or invoice through verification, budget availability check, approval, coding, payment, posting, filing, and reconciliation. For the P4I role, the process specifically covers program expenditure, supplier invoices, petty cash, and vendor accounts. For FHI 360, transactions across district-based activities must be recorded promptly and reviewed for accuracy and completeness. A practical control dashboard should show pending approvals, unpaid invoices, transactions awaiting documents, budget pressure, and unusual or rejected items. The Finance Manager should review exceptions rather than relying only on a post-payment check. That allows program teams to continue operating while keeping financial risks visible.
Send to the team:
- "Please follow the full expenditure workflow and resolve missing approvals, budget checks, coding, or supporting documents before payment."
Key point:
- Speed should come from a clear process, not from weaker controls.

4. Q: How would you ensure that payments comply simultaneously with donor, organizational, and local requirements?
A: The Finance Manager should not treat payment approval as a single signature step. Before payment, the team should verify the expenditure purpose, approved budget, procurement route, delegated authority, supporting documents, tax implications, donor eligibility, vendor information, and accounting classification. The P4I vacancy explicitly requires payments to comply with P4I, DFAT, and DT Global procedures, while the FHI 360 role requires compliance with FHI 360 policies and Indonesia statutes. A compliance matrix can make the review consistent, particularly for higher-risk purchases, consultants, taxes, and exceptions. Where requirements conflict, the issue should be escalated before payment rather than resolved informally. Power Query can test large transaction populations for missing fields or unusual combinations, while VBA can generate pre-payment review checklists for finance staff.
Send to the team:

5. Q: How would you manage petty cash without allowing it to become a weak control area?
A: Petty cash should operate under a fixed or otherwise clearly defined control framework, with one accountable custodian, authorized purposes, sequential vouchers, original receipts, periodic reconciliation, and documented replenishment. The P4I vacancy specifically includes petty cash management and a full and accurate receipt filing system. The Finance Manager should establish a surprise-count process, reconcile physical cash to the recorded balance, investigate differences immediately, and set an appropriate cash ceiling so unnecessary funds are not kept on hand. Any old, unsupported, or unusual petty cash item should be escalated rather than carried forward indefinitely. The control objective is not merely to count the cash. It is to prove that the cash balance, supporting vouchers, and accounting records agree and that access is properly restricted.
Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile petty cash regularly, retain original support, and report every unexplained difference immediately."
Key point:
- Petty cash should have a clear custodian, evidence trail, and reconciliation.
- Surprise counts help test whether recorded balances remain reliable.
Official links:
- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
6. Q: How would you review supplier accounts and accounts payable in a development program?
A: Supplier accounts should be controlled as a complete liability cycle rather than as isolated invoice payments. The Finance Manager should reconcile vendor statements where relevant, review outstanding invoices by age, confirm that services or goods were received, verify approval and procurement evidence, check tax treatment, and ensure liabilities are recorded in the correct period. The P4I vacancy specifically assigns accounts payable functions for suppliers and vendors and requires liaison with service providers so payment, cash-flow, and budget information remain current. A monthly payable aging should distinguish current invoices, disputed items, missing documentation, overdue approved invoices, and items awaiting budget confirmation. This supports both operational responsiveness and cash-flow forecasting. Power Query can merge vendor ledgers and invoice registers, while VBA can produce aging reports and follow-up lists.
Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile significant supplier balances and clearly document why any approved invoice remains unpaid."
Key point:
- Accounts payable is both a liability-control and cash-management process.
- Aging should explain the reason behind every material outstanding balance.

7. Q: How would you manage vendor relationships without compromising financial independence?

The P4I role specifically requires monitoring and liaising with vendors and service providers to keep payments, cash flow, and budgeting information accurate and current. That means finance should confirm balances with vendor information but retain independent control over approval and accounting records. Any disputed amount should be documented, and material disputes should be escalated to the responsible operations or program authority. The relationship should support timely settlement without weakening segregation of duties or procurement controls.

8. Q: How would you manage a program bank account and maintain a reliable bank reconciliation?
A: Bank control should combine transaction authorization, reconciliation, cash forecasting, transfer management, and restricted access. For the P4I role, the Finance Manager is responsible for the Thailand program bank account, including reconciliations, cash-flow forecasting, and funds transfer requests. The reconciliation should compare bank statements with the accounting ledger, identify deposits in transit, outstanding payments, bank charges, transfers, errors, and unexplained items, and ensure reconciling items are cleared promptly. The manager should review whether bank signatories and online access remain appropriate and whether transfer requests are supported by the approved cash requirement. A reconciliation is complete only when every material difference is explained and supported, not merely when the ending balance appears plausible. Power Query can normalize bank and ledger files, while VBA can produce monthly reconciliation packs and aged reconciling-item reports.

9. Q: How would you prepare a cash-flow forecast for a program whose activities can change quickly?
A: Cash-flow forecasting should start with the approved operating plan and then incorporate payment timing, payroll, supplier commitments, expected reimbursements, transfers, tax obligations, and known activity schedules. For P4I, the vacancy explicitly links bank management, cash-flow forecasting, funds transfer requests, and program implementation. The Finance Manager should separate committed cash requirements from less certain forecast items and update the forecast as program priorities move. A useful forecast should show opening cash, expected receipts or transfers, planned payments, timing assumptions, closing cash, and minimum liquidity requirements. Variance analysis should compare prior forecasts to actual cash movements so the assumptions improve over time. Power Query can combine payment registers, payroll schedules, supplier aging, and activity forecasts; VBA can refresh the forecast and identify periods of liquidity pressure.
Send to the team:
- "Please update the cash forecast using current commitments and realistic payment timing rather than relying only on the approved budget."
Key point:
- Cash forecasting is about timing as well as amount.
- Forecast accuracy improves when actual cash outcomes are compared with previous assumptions.
Official links:
- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
10. Q: How would you control funds transfer requests to avoid both liquidity problems and unnecessary idle cash?
A: A transfer request should be supported by a documented cash requirement rather than by a routine calendar alone. The Finance Manager should consider current bank balance, committed payments, payroll, tax obligations, approved activities, expected receipts, and timing of upcoming disbursements. The request should be reviewed against the latest cash-flow forecast and approved under the applicable authority. For P4I, funds transfer requests are directly tied to management of the Thailand program bank account and cash-flow forecasting. The manager should also investigate repeated large transfers followed by prolonged unused balances, because this may indicate forecasting weaknesses or delayed activities. Power Query can compare transfer history with forecast and actual expenditure, while VBA can flag unusually large or unexplained transfer requests for review.
Send to the team:
- "Please support each funds transfer request with a current cash forecast and documented near-term payment requirement."
Key point:
- Transfers should be driven by evidenced liquidity needs.
- Repeated forecasting gaps should lead to process improvement, not simply larger cash buffers.
Official links:
- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
11. Q: How would you link operational planning and budgets so finance information remains useful to program teams?
A: Finance should not treat the budget as a static spreadsheet. The Finance Manager should connect work plans, activity timing, procurement plans, staffing assumptions, expected invoices, and approved funding to a structured budget-monitoring process. The P4I vacancy requires support for forward planning of financial resources, operational budgeting, work plans, and disbursement in line with plans. The FHI 360 role similarly requires budget analysis for project funds and support to technical and procurement teams. A practical monthly review should compare budget, actual expenditure, commitments where available, forecast, and remaining available funds, with explanations for significant deviations. This enables program managers to adjust activities before a variance becomes a compliance or funding problem.
Send to the team:
- "Please review budget, actuals, commitments, and forecast together so financial decisions reflect the current work plan."
Key point:
- Budget management should be linked to operational activity planning.
- Variance review is most useful when it leads to an action or updated forecast.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
12. Q: How would you perform budget-versus-actual analysis for a donor-funded project?
A: The starting point is a budget structure that matches the reporting and operational dimensions used in the accounting system. The Finance Manager should compare approved budget, current approved revisions, actual expenditure, commitments where tracked, forecast to completion, and remaining balance. Variances should be analyzed by project, activity, account, period, and where relevant donor reporting category. A variance is not automatically a problem; the manager needs to distinguish timing differences, planned underspending, genuine efficiency, delayed implementation, incorrect coding, and potential overspending. The FHI 360 role explicitly calls for budget analyses for project funds and explanation of discrepancies and variations. Power Query can consolidate accounting extracts and budget files, while VBA can generate standardized monthly variance reports with threshold-based exception flags.
Send to the team:
- "Please explain material budget variances by cause, timing, corrective action, and forecast impact."
Key point:
- Variance analysis should explain why the number changed and what happens next.
- A good forecast is more useful than simply reporting historical variance.
Official links:
- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
13. Q: How would you investigate a significant financial discrepancy or unexplained variation?
A: The Finance Manager should first define the variance precisely, then trace it to the underlying transactions, coding, period, operational event, or source data. Useful questions include whether the difference is caused by timing, duplicate posting, incorrect account or project coding, an omitted entry, tax treatment, foreign exchange, procurement changes, or an actual operational change. The FHI 360 vacancy specifically requires interpretation and explanation of discrepancies and variations in financial data. The investigation should document the root cause, evidence reviewed, correction required, responsible person, and whether the issue has broader implications. If one error suggests a systemic control weakness, the manager should expand the review rather than correcting only the visible transaction. Power Query is useful for identifying patterns across large datasets, while VBA can compile exception cases for management review.
Send to the team:
- "Please document the cause and corrective action for every material unexplained financial variation."
Key point:
- A discrepancy should be explained, not merely adjusted away.
- Root-cause analysis helps prevent repeated errors across the portfolio.
Official links:
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
14. Q: How would you manage monthly financial close for a donor-funded project?
A: The close process should have a documented calendar covering transaction cut-off, bank reconciliations, payroll, tax liabilities, payables, accruals where needed, project coding review, balance-sheet reconciliations, budget-versus-actual analysis, management review, and final reporting. The FHI 360 vacancy specifically requires monthly financial close reports, including bank reconciliations, for submission to Corporate Accounting. The Finance Manager should establish preparer and reviewer responsibilities and maintain a close checklist showing open items, evidence, review status, and final sign-off. For P4I, monthly reporting also needs reliable records and reconciliations that support DFAT program management. Power Query can assemble source schedules into a standard close model, while VBA can generate the checklist and identify incomplete reconciliations before the reporting pack is finalized.
Send to the team:
- "Please complete the monthly close checklist and clear or formally document all material open items before submission."
Key point:
- A close is a controlled sequence, not simply the production of a report.
- Reconciliations and reviewer sign-off provide the evidence that the reported numbers are dependable.

15. Q: How would you ensure transactions for district-based activities are recorded accurately and on time?
A: A geographically distributed activity portfolio creates a higher risk of delayed documentation, inconsistent coding, and incomplete transaction support. The Finance Manager should establish a standard submission calendar, minimum supporting-document requirements, clear responsibility for review, and a process for correcting rejected transactions. The FHI 360 vacancy specifically requires timely recording of all financial transactions for assigned district-based activities and review for accuracy and completeness. The manager should monitor activity location, project, account, donor, date, amount, and supporting evidence, while escalating repeated delays from the same activity or team. Power Query can combine district transaction files into a centralized review population and identify missing fields or late submissions. Send to the team:
- "Please submit complete district activity documentation on the agreed timetable and resolve rejected or incomplete transactions promptly."
Key point:
- Distributed operations require standardized submission and review controls.

16. Q: How would you review transaction coding to protect project and donor reporting accuracy?
A: Correct amounts can still produce incorrect reports if the account, project, activity, funding source, or other accounting dimensions are wrong. The Finance Manager should maintain approved coding structures and review unusual combinations, manual journal entries, reclassifications, and transactions posted near period end. Before project financial reports are issued, the team should reconcile accounting data to the approved budget structure and investigate transactions sitting in suspense or miscellaneous categories. This is particularly relevant to FHI 360 district-based activities and to donor-funded P4I reporting. A master coding table can be used to validate allowed project-account combinations. Power Query can test transaction populations against the master list and isolate unmatched codes, while VBA can create a coding review report before monthly close.
Send to the team:
- "Please validate project, activity, account, and donor coding before finalizing monthly financial reports."
Key point:
- Classification errors can be as consequential as amount errors.
- Master coding tables provide a repeatable validation control.

17. Q: How would you manage local payroll and ensure deductions are correctly accounted for?
A: Payroll should reconcile employee records, approved salary information, attendance or other relevant inputs, gross pay, deductions, employer obligations where applicable, net pay, bank payment, payroll liabilities, and subsequent statutory remittances. The P4I vacancy specifically requires operation of monthly payroll for locally employed staff, including tax and other deductions, while the technical criteria emphasize end-to-end payroll processing and Thailand taxation and labour law. The Finance Manager should separate payroll preparation from final authorization, reconcile payroll to the general ledger, investigate unusual month-on-month movements, and retain approved payroll documentation securely. A payroll control schedule should show the total payroll liability, deductions, amounts paid, and amounts remitted. VBA can produce payroll reconciliation checklists and exception reports, while Power Query can compare payroll outputs with accounting postings.
Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile gross payroll, deductions, net payment, ledger posting, and statutory remittances before closing the payroll cycle."
Key point:
- Payroll control is a chain from authorized employee data to payment and statutory settlement.
- Reconciliation protects both financial accuracy and legal compliance.

18. Q: How would you ensure payroll taxes and other employee deductions are remitted on time?
A: The Finance Manager should maintain a statutory calendar linked to payroll processing, showing the type of obligation, applicable period, amount calculated, filing or payment deadline, responsible preparer, reviewer, and evidence of submission or payment. The P4I role requires payroll deductions and timely tax and other contributions, while its selection criteria specifically emphasize knowledge of local taxation and labour laws. The control should reconcile the payroll liability account to actual remittances and investigate old balances. Late or inaccurate remittances can produce penalties, reporting problems, employee disputes, and reputational risk. Power Query can combine payroll calculations with payment records and statutory schedules; VBA can flag approaching deadlines and unresolved liability balances.
Send to the team:
- "Please reconcile every payroll-related liability to the corresponding filing and payment evidence before the statutory deadline."
Key point:
- Statutory payroll obligations require a dated compliance workflow.
- Liability reconciliation helps prevent both missed payments and unexplained old balances.

19. Q: How would you manage tax compliance for a project that has both employee and non-employee payments?
A: Tax review should be embedded in the transaction process rather than performed only after payments are made. The FHI 360 vacancy specifically assigns responsibility for tax calculation and compliance for the EPIC project, including tax calculation for non-employees. The Finance Manager should therefore establish a payment tax matrix covering employee payroll, consultant and service-provider payments, and other relevant transaction types, with the applicable calculation basis, documentation, review responsibility, and remittance requirement. Non-employee payments should be reviewed before payment so the correct withholding or other treatment is applied rather than attempting to recover errors later. The manager should reconcile tax liabilities to filings and payments, retain working papers, and monitor changes in applicable regulations. Power Query can categorize payment populations for tax testing, while VBA can generate tax-review schedules from approved transaction data.

Key point:
- Tax compliance starts at transaction classification.
- Non-employee payments need explicit review rather than being treated like ordinary supplier invoices.

31. Q: How would you use spreadsheets safely for financial analysis and reporting?

The FHI 360 role requires financial models and analysis of financial data, while P4I emphasizes forward planning and budgeting. 35. Q: How would you distinguish a timing variance from a genuine budget problem?
A: The Finance Manager should compare the original activity plan, current implementation status, payment timing, contractual commitments, actual expenditure, and updated forecast. A program may appear significantly underspent because a procurement process is delayed even though the activity is still expected to proceed. Conversely, a persistent underspend may indicate that the planned activity will not be delivered as originally designed, creating a real resource-allocation issue. The P4I vacancy connects budgeting with operational work plans and disbursement, while FHI 360 requires budget analysis and explanation of financial variations. The manager should document the cause, expected resolution, and impact on year-to-date and project-to-complete forecasts. This prevents temporary timing differences from being confused with structural budget variance.
Send to the team:
- "Please explain significant underspending by activity status and expected timing before treating it as a permanent budget saving."
Key point:
- Timing variance and true budget variance require different management responses.
- Forecast-to-completion is essential for interpreting current underspending.

37. Q: How would you respond to an audit finding that identifies a weakness in the finance process?
A: The Finance Manager should distinguish the factual finding from the recommended corrective action and determine whether the issue is isolated or systemic. The response should identify the root cause, control gap, responsible owner, action required, target date, and evidence that will demonstrate completion. The P4I role specifically requires progression of audit recommendations that improve financial processes and controls. The Finance Manager should monitor implementation rather than treating the response letter as the end of the matter. Where appropriate, the revised control should be tested after implementation to confirm that the weakness has actually been addressed. A corrective-action tracker can show status, evidence, and management escalation. VBA can generate overdue action alerts and management summaries without changing the underlying audit evidence.
Send to the team:
- "Please record every agreed audit action with an owner, deadline, corrective measure, and evidence of closure."
Key point:
- Audit recommendations should result in measurable control improvements.
- Closure requires evidence that the new control is operating, not only a written promise.

38. Q: How would you use internal audit or control testing to identify weaknesses before they become material problems?
A: Control testing should focus on the controls that address the program's most significant financial risks. Examples include payment authorization, bank reconciliations, payroll deductions, tax remittances, project coding, supporting documentation, and donor-compliance requirements. The Finance Manager should define the population, select a reasonable sample or analytical test, document exceptions, assess whether the issue is isolated, and determine whether corrective action is needed. The FHI 360 role's requirement to support compliance and develop finance procedures and the P4I requirement to improve processes through audit recommendations both support this preventive approach. Power Query can test large populations for selected control attributes, while VBA can maintain testing logs and produce management summaries.
Send to the team:
- "Please test high-risk controls periodically and document both the exceptions found and the corrective action taken."
Key point:
- Control testing provides evidence that procedures operate in practice.
- The value of testing is greater when exceptions trigger corrective action and follow-up.

- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
39. Q: How would you identify fraud or leakage risks within routine financial operations?
A: Fraud-risk review should focus on situations where authorization, custody, recording, and review are insufficiently separated or where transactions can be concealed within normal activity. Relevant warning signs include duplicate vendors or payments, unusual payment timing, repeated round amounts, unexplained manual journals, long-outstanding advances, unsupported adjustments, frequent exceptions, and inappropriate access to financial systems or bank accounts. The Finance Manager should reinforce segregation of duties, independent review, documented approvals, and exception monitoring while escalating suspected issues through the organization's established process. P4I's posting explicitly highlights zero tolerance for fraud, corruption, bribery, and related risks, while FHI 360 also includes integrity and compliance expectations. Power Query can flag analytical anomalies, but investigation and escalation require human judgment and documented evidence.
Send to the team:
- "Please report suspected fraud or unexplained control breaches through the approved escalation process and do not alter evidence during an investigation."
Key point:
- Fraud prevention depends on multiple complementary controls.
- Analytical flags can identify cases for review but do not replace investigation procedures.
Official links:
- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
40. Q: How would you maintain segregation of duties in a small program finance team?
A: Small teams require deliberate design because one employee may perform several related tasks. The Finance Manager should map who can initiate, review, approve, record, pay, reconcile, and modify financial information, then identify incompatible combinations that need compensating controls. For example, the person preparing a bank reconciliation should not be the only person with authority to approve bank payments or investigate unmatched items. Similar care is needed for vendor setup, payment preparation, payroll, and tax remittances. Where full separation is not practical, an independent managerial review should be documented. The P4I and FHI 360 positions both emphasize financial oversight and accuracy, making clear allocation of control responsibilities a central management task rather than an administrative detail.
Send to the team:
- "Please review user access and transaction responsibilities periodically to confirm incompatible duties are not concentrated with one person."
Key point:
- Segregation of duties should be designed around risk, not headcount alone.
- Compensating reviews must be documented when full separation is impossible.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
41. Q: How would you manage competing finance priorities when reporting deadlines and operational demands occur at the same time?
A: The Finance Manager should prioritize based on financial risk, statutory or donor deadlines, liquidity implications, management dependency, and the possibility of irreversible consequences. The P4I vacancy explicitly requires strong organization, prioritization, delivery under tight deadlines, and flexibility when program priorities change. A practical workload plan should distinguish critical deadline-driven tasks from work that can be batched or delegated. The manager should maintain a visible close and compliance calendar, assign clear owners, and escalate emerging delays early. Automation can reduce time spent on repeatable reconciliation and reporting tasks, allowing senior staff to focus on judgmental issues. VBA can sequence standard close outputs and reminders, while Power Query can refresh recurring analysis without repeated manual copying.
Send to the team:
- "Please prioritize finance work by deadline, financial risk, compliance impact, and dependency on other teams, and escalate conflicts early."
Key point:
- Prioritization should be risk-based rather than driven only by whoever asks first.
- Automation creates capacity for review and decision making, not permission to remove controls.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
42. Q: How would you communicate a financial risk to senior management without losing the technical detail needed for action?
A: A Finance Manager should separate the accounting fact, financial impact, cause, risk, proposed response, owner, and deadline. For example, rather than reporting only that expenditure is below budget, the manager should explain whether the variance reflects delayed procurement, a changed activity plan, a likely saving, or a risk that funds will not be utilized as expected. The P4I role emphasizes proactive management and communication of risks and issues, while the FHI 360 role requires interaction with staff, officials, executives, and written articulation of complex issues. Management reporting should therefore use concise decision-oriented summaries with supporting schedules available for review. Power Query can maintain the analytical source, while VBA can generate consistent management summaries from controlled data.
Send to the team:
- "Please report financial issues with the amount, cause, risk, proposed action, owner, and deadline clearly identified."
Key point:
- Senior management needs actionable financial information, not unexplained figures.
- Technical detail should remain available in supporting schedules even when the headline message is concise.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
43. Q: How would you document a complex accounting or compliance issue so that another reviewer can understand the conclusion?
A: A complex issue should be documented as a short decision memo or structured working paper. It should explain the transaction or problem, relevant policy or requirement, facts established, alternatives considered, analysis performed, conclusion, accounting or process impact, approvals required, and evidence retained. The FHI 360 vacancy specifically requires documenting complex issues and effectively articulating written conclusions. The P4I role similarly requires excellent record keeping for audit and donor management. This approach prevents the organization from relying on undocumented institutional memory. The file should be linked to the relevant transaction or reporting period and retained according to the applicable record requirements. A controlled template can be automated with VBA for consistency, while Power Query can provide the underlying transaction population used in the analysis.
Send to the team:
- "Please document material accounting judgments and complex compliance issues in a structured working paper before finalizing the treatment."
Key point:
- Written conclusions preserve institutional knowledge and audit evidence.
- A good memo records both the facts and the reasoning behind the decision.
Official links:
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
44. Q: How would you monitor financial performance across a program while keeping the management report understandable?
A: The Finance Manager should identify a limited set of decision-useful measures such as expenditure against budget, forecast-to-completion, cash position, outstanding supplier liabilities, unresolved reconciliations, tax obligations, aged exceptions, and major activity variances. The P4I role requires accurate monthly financial reporting and budget-versus-actual updates, while FHI 360 requires ongoing analysis and financial reporting for project funds. A management report should not merely reproduce the general ledger. It should explain the major movements and highlight decisions required. Power Query can feed standardized indicators from accounting and operational sources, while VBA can produce a monthly dashboard and exception summary without changing the source data. The underlying detailed schedules should remain available for review.
Send to the team:
- "Please keep the monthly management report focused on material variances, cash, commitments, risks, and actions required."
Key point:
- A useful management report connects financial data to decisions.
- Detailed supporting schedules should sit behind a concise management view.

45. Q: How would you maintain financial continuity when a key finance staff member is absent or leaves the program?
A: Financial continuity depends on documented processes, current trackers, controlled access, standardized reconciliations, and evidence that another trained person can understand the status of open work. The P4I vacancy emphasizes working effectively with limited supervision, while FHI 360 includes supervision and maintenance of project finance records. The Finance Manager should ensure that payment calendars, reconciliation files, tax schedules, close checklists, policy references, vendor information, and unresolved exceptions are up to date and stored in accessible controlled locations. Critical processes should have at least one trained backup. VBA can standardize recurring checklists and reporting outputs, while Power Query can reduce dependence on manual data transformations known only to one individual.
Send to the team:
- "Please keep process notes, reconciliations, trackers, and open-action lists current so another trained finance staff member can continue the process without reconstructing the history."
Key point:
- Continuity is a control outcome of documentation and cross-training.
- Automation should reduce single-person dependency without removing review responsibilities.

46. Q: How would you ensure finance staff understand the connection between accounting records and program delivery?
A: Finance should explain how transactions relate to activities, budgets, procurement, staffing, donor requirements, and cash timing. The P4I vacancy places the Finance Manager close to Operations, while the FHI 360 role requires close work with technical and procurement teams. This means finance should not review transactions in isolation. A program expenditure report should answer what activity the spending supported, whether it was planned, whether the cost is correctly coded, whether sufficient funding remains, and whether the documentation supports the transaction. Regular discussions with program teams can identify emerging risks before they appear in the ledger. Power Query can connect activity and transaction datasets where reliable identifiers exist, allowing finance to analyze expenditure by activity and timing without manual re-keying.
Send to the team:
- "Please include the activity purpose and financial implication when discussing significant transactions or budget changes with finance."
Key point:
- Program finance works best when accounting information is connected to operational decisions.
- Shared identifiers improve the ability to connect activity and financial data.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I

47. Q: How would you use financial data to identify emerging risk rather than simply report past performance?
A: A Finance Manager should monitor trends that suggest future pressure, such as declining cash coverage, recurring budget variances, accelerating commitments, delayed acquittals, unusual tax balances, growing outstanding invoices, or repeated documentation failures. Historical reporting can show what happened, but management needs forward-looking interpretation. P4I's requirement for forward planning, cash-flow forecasting, and proactive risk communication supports this approach, while FHI 360's requirement for analysis and interpretation of discrepancies supports ongoing monitoring. Power Query can calculate trends across monthly datasets and identify exception conditions, while VBA can produce an escalation dashboard. The manager should then connect each flagged trend to a responsible owner and agreed response rather than treating the alert as a report in itself.
Send to the team:
- "Please review significant financial trends each month and document the operational action required when a risk indicator moves outside its expected range."
Key point:
- Financial analysis should help predict pressure, not only describe historical results.
- Every meaningful risk indicator should connect to a management response.

- Finance Manager — DT Global, P4I
- Accounting and Finance Officer — FHI 360
48. Q: How would you design an automated finance review process using Power Query and VBA without automating management judgment?
A: Automation should handle repetitive preparation, consolidation, validation, and exception identification while leaving accounting judgments, approval decisions, and escalations with authorized staff. A practical model could use Power Query to import accounting exports, bank data, payroll summaries, supplier listings, budget files, tax schedules, document trackers, and activity data into standardized tables. The query layer can normalize formats, test required fields, identify duplicates, compare balances, and create exception tables. VBA can then refresh approved queries, produce close checklists, generate reconciliation reports, create overdue-action lists, and archive controlled outputs. The Finance Manager should build validation checks so the process stops or clearly flags problems when source data is missing, stale, duplicated, or structurally changed. This fits both vacancies because they emphasize financial systems, Excel, reconciliations, reporting, controls, record keeping, and process improvement. Automation should reduce manual repetition while preserving independent review and approval.
Send to the team:
- "Please automate repeatable reconciliation and reporting steps, but require documented finance review for exceptions, accounting judgments, approvals, and final submissions."
Key point:
- Power Query is most useful for controlled data preparation and analysis, while VBA is most useful for repeatable workflow and output tasks.
- The control model should automate the routine and preserve human judgment for exceptions and approvals.
